OFF-ROAD VEHICLES
§ 73.001 DEFINITION.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definition shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   OFF-ROAD VEHICLE. Shall have the definition prescribed in I.C. 9-13-2-117.3
(I.C. 14-8-2-185), as amended, and must be capable of registration under I.C.
9-18.1-14. The term OFF-ROAD VEHICLE refers to a vehicle manufactured with a
standard steering wheel for operation, side-by-side seating arrangement for
passengers, seat belts and roll over protection in accordance with I.C. 14-19-
1-0.5 (motorized cart). The term OFF-ROAD VEHICLE does not include motorcycles,
motor scooters, all-terrain vehicles, snowmobiles or any vehicle capable of
being registered and licensed by the state.
(Ord. 5221, passed 5-20-2025)
§ 73.002 OPERATION OF OFF-ROAD VEHICLES.
   (A)   The operation of off-road vehicles upon highways under the
jurisdiction of the city is strictly prohibited unless the off-road vehicle is
operated and equipped in full compliance with this subchapter.
   (B)   This subchapter does not restrict the use of municipally-owned off-
road vehicles used for maintenance, public safety or special events.
(Ord. 5221, passed 5-20-2025) Penalty, see §
73.999
§ 73.003 REQUIREMENTS FOR OPERATION OF OFF-ROAD VEHICLES.
   (A)   Rules.
      (1)   A person who operates an off-road vehicle on a highway under the
jurisdiction of the city must comply with all federal, state and local traffic
laws as if the off-road vehicle were a passenger motor vehicle, as defined in
I.C. 9-13-2-123. Off-road vehicles may not be operated in a manner that
endangers life or property.
      (2)   No person may operate an off-road vehicle on highways under the
jurisdiction of the city unless all passengers of said vehicle are seated in a
factory installed seat position attached to the vehicle, with seatbelt
restraints properly fastened as specified by the manufacturer.
      (3)   An off-road vehicle which is only operated at 25 mph or less shall
display the slow-moving vehicle emblem described by I.C. 9-21-9-2.
      (4)   All occupants within the off-road vehicle less than 18 years of age
must wear a helmet per I.C. 9-18.1-14-11, except as permitted by state law.
   (B)   Equipment.
      (1)   Off-road vehicles must be equipped with the minimum safety
equipment as set forth in I.C. 14-16-1, as amended from time to time, in order
to be registered and operated on highways under the jurisdiction of the city.
      (2)   All safety equipment must be maintained in good operating order.
Lights must be used at
all times in order for the off-road vehicle to be visible from a distance of at
least 500 feet.
      (3)   Failure to equip, maintain and use such equipment as required
herein shall constitute a violation of this subchapter.
   (C)   Operator.
      (1)   Only individuals 16 years of age or older, who have been issued a
driver’s license from a State Bureau of Motor Vehicles, and whose driver’s
license is not suspended or revoked, may operate an off-road vehicle on a
highway under the jurisdiction of the city.
      (2)   The owner of a off-road vehicle may not cause or knowingly permit
an individual to operate the off-road vehicle on highways under the
jurisdiction of the city unless the individual holds a valid driver’s license
from a State Bureau of Motor Vehicles that is not suspended or revoked.
   (D)   Financial responsibility.
      (1)   A person shall not operate an off-road vehicle on a highway under
the jurisdiction of the city without current financial responsibility in effect
on said vehicle in accordance with I.C. 9-25-4-4, as amended from time to time.
      (2)   A person who operates an off-road vehicle on a highway under the
jurisdiction of the city must, at all times, maintain the state required
minimum amount of financial responsibility in accordance with I.C. 9-25-4-5, as
amended from time to time.
   (E)   Highways under the jurisdiction of the city. Off-road vehicles shall
not be operated on any bicycle path, trail, sidewalk or any public property
within the city that is not designated for vehicular traffic.
(Ord. 5221, passed 5-20-2025) Penalty, see §
73.999
§ 73.004 PERMITS.
   (A)   No off-road vehicle may be operated within the city unless the owner
of such off-road vehicle shall have applied for and obtained a valid off-road
vehicle registration from the state per I.C. 9-18.1-14.
   (B)   The State Bureau of Motor Vehicles issues a certificate of
registration and two decals for each off-road vehicle and registration. The
certificate of registration must accompany the off-road vehicle and be made
available for inspection upon demand by a police officer. The decals contain
the off-road vehicle’s registration number and expiration date and must be
attached on the forward half of the off-road vehicle.
   (C)   No city permit, decal or inspection is required of off-road vehicles
registered through the State Bureau of Motor Vehicles as prescribed by I.C. 9-
18.1-14.
(Ord. 5221, passed 5-20-2025) Penalty, see §
73.999
GOLF CARTS
§ 73.015 DEFINITION.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definition shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   GOLF CART. Shall have the definition prescribed in I.C. 9-13-2-69.7, as
amended. The term GOLF CART means a motor vehicle designed and manufactured for
operation on a golf course for sporting or recreational purposes that is not
capable of exceeding speeds of 25 mph.
(Ord. 5192, passed 7-11-2024)
§ 73.016 OPERATION OF GOLF CARTS.
   (A)   The operation of a golf cart upon highways under the jurisdiction of
the city is strictly prohibited, unless the golf cart is operated and equipped
in full compliance with this subchapter.
   (B)   This subchapter does not restrict the use of municipally-owned golf
carts used for maintenance, public safety or special events.
(Ord. 5192, passed 7-11-2024) Penalty, see §
73.999
§ 73.017 REQUIREMENTS FOR OPERATION OF GOLF CARTS.
   (A)   Rules.
      (1)   A person who operates a golf cart on a highway under the
jurisdiction of the city must comply with all federal, state and local traffic
laws.
      (2)   A person who operates a golf cart on a highway under the
jurisdiction of the city must comply with slow-moving vehicle regulations under
I.C. 9-21-9, as amended from time to time, including the requirement that such
vehicle properly display a slow-moving vehicle emblem.
      (3)   No golf cart may be operated on a highway under the jurisdiction of
the city in excess of 25 mph.
      (4)   No person may operate a golf cart on highways under the
jurisdiction of the city unless all passengers of said vehicle are seated in a
factory installed seat position attached to the vehicle, with seatbelt
restraints properly fastened as specified by the manufacturer.
      (5)   A golf cart may not be operated in a manner that unreasonably
impedes the normal flow of traffic, and may not be operated in a manner that
endangers life or property.
   (B)   Equipment.
      (1)   Unless otherwise exempted herein, all golf carts must be equipped
consistent with I.C. 9-21-9-4, as amended from time to time, with the following
minimum safety equipment in order to be permitted and operated on highways
under the jurisdiction of the city:
         (a)   A rear-view mirror;
         (b)   Head lights;
         (c)   Tail lights;
         (d)   Brake lights;
         (e)   Turn signals (front and back);
         (f)   Factory installed seat belt restraints or DOT-approved after-
market installed belt restraints for all seating positions;
         (g)   Have installed an ATV/UTV orange safety flag; and
         (h)   Slow moving vehicle emblem.
      (2)   All safety equipment must be maintained in good operating order.
Lights must be used at all times. Failure to equip, maintain and use such
equipment as required herein shall constitute a violation of this subchapter.
   (C)   Operator.
      (1)   Only individuals 16 years of age or older, who have been issued a
driver’s license from a State Bureau of Motor Vehicles, and whose driver’s
license is not suspended or revoked, may operate a golf cart on a highway under
the jurisdiction of the city.
      (2)   The owner of a golf cart may not cause or knowingly permit an
individual to operate the golf cart on highways under the jurisdiction of the
city unless the individual holds a valid driver’s license from a State Bureau
of Motor Vehicles that is not suspended or revoked.
   (D)   Financial responsibility.
      (1)   A person shall not operate a golf cart on a highway under the
jurisdiction of the city without current financial responsibility in effect on
said vehicle in accordance with I.C. 9-25-4-4, as amended from time to time.
      (2)   A person who operates a golf cart on a highway under the
jurisdiction of the city must, at all times, maintain the state required
minimum amount of financial responsibility in accordance with I.C. 9-25-4-5, as
amended from time to time.
   (E)   Highways under the jurisdiction of the city.
      (1)   Golf carts shall not be operated on any bicycle path, trail,
sidewalk or any public property within the city that is not designated for
vehicular traffic.
      (2)   Golf carts shall not be operated on any highway within the
corporate boundaries of the city that is designated as part of the State
Highway System (e.g., S.R. 119, S.R. 15, S.R. 4 and U.S. 33). (See I.C. 9-21-1-
3.3.)
      (3)   Golf carts may be operated on any highway within the corporate
boundaries of the city that has a posted speed limit of 30 mph or less, and
highways that do not have a posted speed limit, but have a speed limit of 30
mph or less as prescribed by state statute.
      (4)   Golf carts shall not be operated on any highway within the
corporate boundaries of the city that has a posted speed limit of 35 mph or
more, and highways that do not have a posted speed limit, but have a speed
limit of 35 mph or more as prescribed by state statute.
      (5)   In addition, golf carts are permitted to cross highways, on which
they are otherwise not permitted to operate, at right angles in order to travel
from one highway to another highway when the operation can be done safely.
(Ord. 5192, passed 7-11-2024) Penalty, see §
73.999
§ 73.018 PERMIT.
   (A)   (1)   No golf cart may be operated within the city, unless the owner
of such golf cart shall have applied for and obtained a valid golf cart permit
from the city.
      (2)   This excludes golf carts owned by golf courses, located within the
city and utilized for operation on a golf course for sporting or recreational
purposes.
   (B)   Application for such a permit must be made utilizing the application
form as approved from time to time by the Board of Public Works and Safety.
   (C)   The fee for such application shall be $50, payable to the city. Such
permit shall have an effective length of the applicant’s ownership of the
vehicle.
   (D)   Issuance of said permit must include visual inspection of the golf
cart during normal business hours by a member of the City Police Department or
the City Fire Department to determine compliance.
   (E)   Upon issuance of a permit, the golf cart owner shall be provided a
decal, which must be affixed to the left rear of the vehicle at all times so as
to be visible from the rear of the vehicle.
   (F)   A person or entity may apply for a temporary permit for the use of a
golf cart during or in connection with an authorized parade or festival. Any
petition for a temporary permit must be presented to the Board of Public Works
and Safety, which shall have the authority to determine if a temporary permit
for a specific use and limitations shall be issued, and to issue said temporary
permit. Any issuance of temporary permits with limitations approved by Board of
Public Works and Safety will have an effective period not longer than five days
from the effective start date of the temporary permit, as determined by the
Board of Public Works and Safety. The temporary permit and/or paperwork must
accompany the golf cart at all times of use.
(Ord. 5192, passed 7-11-2024) Penalty, see §
73.999
ELECTRIC FOOT SCOOTERS
§ 73.030 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   ELECTRIC FOOT SCOOTER. Shall have the meaning under I.C. 9-13-2-49.4, which
shall include a device:
      (1)   Weighing not more than 100 pounds;
      (2)   Designed to travel on not more than three wheels in contact with
the ground;
      (3)   With handlebars and a floorboard that the rider uses to stand on
the device during operation; and
      (4)   Powered by an electric motor that is capable of powering the device
with or without human propulsion at a speed not more than 20 mph on a paved
level surface, but does not include a motor driven cycle, motor vehicle or
motorcycle.
   SHARED-USE ELECTRIC FOOT SCOOTER OPERATOR. An individual or entity that
leases, rents or otherwise makes available shared-use electric foot scooters,
whether or not for profit. The term does not include individual, private owners
of electric foot scooters who are not engaged in the business or renting,
leasing or otherwise making electric foot scooters available to members of the
public at large.
   SHARED-USE ELECTRIC FOOT SCOOTERS. An electric foot scooter placed in
service by a shared-use electric foot scooter operator.
(Ord. 5121, passed 5-16-2022)
§ 73.031 SCOPE.
   This subchapter shall apply to shared-use electric foot scooter operators
and the use of electric foot scooters.
(Ord. 5121, passed 5-16-2022)
§ 73.032 STATE LAWS.
   Every person who operates an electric foot scooter upon any street, road,
trail, path or sidewalk shall be subject to state and local laws concerning
electric foot scooters and bicycles, generally. Every person who operates an
electric foot scooter shall be subject to all city ordinances except where the
nature of electric foot scooters renders said ordinances inapplicable to
electric foot scooter users.
(Ord. 5121, passed 5-16-2022) Penalty, see §
73.999
§ 73.033 LICENSE.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful to operate as a shared-use electric foot scooter
operator within the city’s corporate boundaries without first obtaining a
license from the City Board of Public Works and Safety.
   (B)   The city may issue a license to a qualified shared-use electric foot
scooter operator for operation of an electric foot scooter system or service
pursuant to any additional terms and conditions established by the Board of
Public Works and Safety. The city reserves the right to issue one or more
electric foot scooter system permits and may, from time to time, limit or
expand the number of permits issued.
   (C)   Each shared-use electric foot scooter operator shall pay any license
fee established by the Common Council. License fees may be updated from time to
time in order to properly reflect the city’s expenses associated with allowing
shared-use electric foot scooters in the public right-of-way.
   (D)   Licenses shall expire one year after issuance. In order to continue
operating, a shared-use electric foot scooter operator shall apply for a new
license at least 30 days prior to the expiration of its existing license and
pay any license fee established for renewal licenses, which may or may not be
the same as the fees set forth for first-time licensees.
   (E)   An application for a shared-use electric foot scooter license shall be
made to the City Board of Public Works and Safety, shall be signed by an
authorized representative of the shared-use electric foot scooter operator and
shall include, at a minimum, the following information:
      (1)   The maximum number of shared-use electric foot scooters the shared-
use electric foot scooter operator may deploy within the corporate boundaries
of the city during the applicable license term;
      (2)   A description of all shared-use electric foot scooters that will be
deployed, including the model, manufacturer and color of each shared-use
electric foot scooter;
      (3)   Color photographs depicting the shared-use electric foot scooter
(s);
      (4)   A schedule of rates and charges that the applicant will charge to
users;
      (5)   Detailed staffing and operational plans, including information
regarding:
         (a)   Local staffing; and
         (b)   Contractors the shared-use electric foot scooter operator will
utilize to perform services related to its shared-use electric foot scooters.
      (6)   A GPS- or GIS-based map depicting the proposed service area of the
shared-use electric foot scooters;
      (7)   A 24-hour customer service telephone number through which users and
members of the public may contact the shared-use electric foot scooter operator
and that will be displayed on each shared-use electric foot scooter;
      (8)   A signed agreement indemnifying the city on a form provided by the
City Board of Public Works and Safety;
      (9)   Proof of all insurance required by the City Board of Public Works
and Safety, including, if required, a bond;
      (10)   Location of all proposed docking stations with a description of
how charging equipment will be installed and maintained. Include an agreement
with any private property owners who will be a host location for docking
stations;
      (11)   The content in its mobile application; the content of its city-
specific webpage;
      (12)   Experience providing services as a shared-use electric foot
scooter operator; and
      (13)   Any other information deemed necessary by the City Board of Public
Works and Safety.
   (F)   Upon receipt of a completed license application and any required
supporting documentation, the City Board of Public Works and Safety shall
either issue or deny the application for a license during a public meeting. In
making this determination, the City Board of Public Works and Safety shall,
among other factors, consider the extent to which the applicant’s prior or
ongoing operations have complied with this subchapter and other applicable law.
   (G)   A shared-use electric foot scooter operator that has obtained a
license shall be permitted to deploy, at a maximum, the number of shared-use
electric foot scooters authorized by the City Board of Public Works and Safety.
During license renewal, the City Board of Public Works and Safety may increase
or decrease the number of scooters a shared-use electric foot scooter operator
is permitted to deploy.
   (H)   If the City Board of Public Works and Safety denies an application
submitted by a shared-use electric foot scooter operator, the Board shall issue
written findings indicating the reasons for said denial.
   (I)   Licenses issued to a shared-use electric foot scooter operator shall
not be assigned or transferred without written city approval. A shared-use
electric foot scooter operator may not subcontract with any other individual,
firm, company or corporation to provide electric foot scooter services within
the city.
(Ord. 5121, passed 5-16-2022) Penalty, see §
73.999
§ 73.034 PARKING ELECTRIC FOOT SCOOTERS.
   (A)   During such times when an a shared-use electric foot scooter is not
actively rented, it must be parked and stored at a docking station designated
by the shared-use electric foot scooter operator. Shared-use electric foot
scooters shall be returned to and parked in designated docking stations, or
otherwise returned to the shared-use electric foot scooter operator at the end
of each rental.
   (B)   Electric foot scooters shall not be parked upon any street, on-street
bicycle paths, multi-use paths or alley, but may be parked in bicycle racks or
upon sidewalks with the following limitations.
      (1)   Electric foot scooters shall be parked in an upright position.
      (2)   Electric foot scooters must be parked in a manner that does not
create a threat to public safety, and does not obstruct, block or interfere
with the use of:
         (a)   Pedestrian movement on a sidewalk;
         (b)   Vehicle parking spaces or loading zones;
         (c)   A curb ramp;
         (d)   Any public transportation infrastructure, including, but not
limited to, bus shelters, bus stop signs or passenger waiting areas;
         (e)   Any business or residential doorway, entrance, exit or driveway;
         (f)   A fire hydrant or other emergency facility;
         (g)   A utility pole or utility box;
         (h)   Street furniture;
         (i)   A commercial window display; or
         (j)   A crosswalk button.
      (3)   Electric foot scooters may not be parked in a manner that impedes
accessibility under the Americans with Disabilities Act, being 42 U.S.C. §§
12101-12213, impedes access to accessible parking zones or impedes access to
parking spaces designated for persons with disabilities.
      (4)   While parked on sidewalks, the location of an electric foot scooter
must leave a clear straight pathway of at least 54 inches wide. The 54 inches
shall not include the width of the curb.
   (C)   The city may move any shared-use electric foot scooter that is parked
in violation of this section to a location where the scooter may be lawfully
parked. If the city opts to move an illegally parked shared-use electric foot
scooter to a legal parking position, the city may nonetheless assess any
applicable fines in accordance with this subchapter.
   (D)   Shared-use electric foot scooter operators will be responsible for
identifying the riders consistently violating rules in regard to parking and
following up with appropriate disciplinary action of increasing severity,
including additional education, warnings, fines and eventual removal of users
from operator apps. Operators must charge an additional fee to any rider not
returning the electric foot scooter to a designated docking station at the end
of the rental period.
(Ord. 5121, passed 5-16-2022) Penalty, see §
73.999
§ 73.035 ELECTRIC FOOT SCOOTER USE.
   Every person who operates an electric foot scooter on public property or
public right-of-way shall comply with the following provisions.
   (A)   A person operating an electric foot scooter shall comply with all
generally applicable traffic and parking laws of the state and city, including
those provisions applicable to the operation of a bicycle.
   (B)   Electric foot scooter shall have no more than one rider at any given
time. Operating a shared-use electric foot scooter with more than one rider
shall constitute a violation.
   (C)   Electric foot scooter shall be permitted on all roads, on-street
bicycle paths and multi-use paths where they exist where the operation of a
bicycle is permitted. Electric foot scooter shall utilize on-street bicycle
paths or multi-use paths where they exist, unless there are signs or markings
indicating that use of electric foot scooter is prohibited.
   (D)   Use of electric foot scooter must not create conditions which are a
threat to public safety and security.
   (E)   A person operating an electric foot scooter shall yield the right-of-
way to any pedestrian.
   (F)   A person operating an electric foot scooter shall give an audible
signal by voice, bell or similar signal before overtaking and passing any
pedestrian while traveling in the same direction and on the same facility as
the pedestrian.
   (G)   No person shall operate an electric foot scooter while controlling an
animal, whether such control is by hand, by leash or by any alternative medium.
   (H)   No person shall operate an electric foot scooter within the designated
boundaries of a special event for which a special event permit has been
obtained.
(Ord. 5121, passed 5-16-2022) Penalty, see §
73.999
§ 73.036 SHARED-USE ELECTRIC FOOT SCOOTER EQUIPMENT, USE, DOCKING STATION,
SAFETY, CONDITION, APPEARANCE AND HOURS OF DEPLOYMENT.
   (A)   Every shared-use electric foot scooter shall conform to all applicable
federal, state and local laws applicable to the design and operation of
electric foot scooters.
   (B)   Shared-use electric foot scooters may only be operated in the service
area approved by the City Board of Public Works and Safety.
   (C)   Shared-use electric foot scooters shall be equipped so as to operate
at a speed no greater than 20 mph.
   (D)   Shared-use electric foot scooters shall be equipped with real-time
location and tracking capability, and with the ability to be geo-fenced to
ensure that each shared-use electric foot scooter is operated and parked
properly.
   (E)   Shared-use electric foot scooter operators shall display the following
items on each shared-use electric foot scooter:
      (1)   A unique identification number visible to the user and to nearby
pedestrians;
      (2)   The required 24-hour telephone number at which members of the
public may contact the shared-use electric foot scooter operator;
      (3)   The shared-use electric foot scooter operator’s website; and
      (4)   Mobile application information of the shared-use electric foot
scooter operator.
   (F)   The software application used by shared-use electric foot scooter
operators shall notify the user of the following items:
      (1)   That users are encouraged to wear helmets;
      (2)   That users are required to obey all traffic laws;
      (3)   That users shall yield to pedestrians in the right-of-way; and
      (4)   That users must follow proper parking procedures.
   (G)   All shared-use electric foot scooters shall be equipped with the
following:
      (1)   A bell, horn or other lawful audible signaling device;
      (2)   A lamp on the front exhibiting a white light visible from a
distance of at least 500 feet to the front;
      (3)   A lamp on the rear exhibiting a red light visible from a distance
of at least 500 feet to the rear or a red reflector visible from a distance of
at least 500 feet to the rear;
      (4)   A kickstand capable of keeping the scooter upright when not in use;
and
      (5)   A brake enabling a person who operates a shared-use electric foot
scooter to make the braked wheels skid on dry, level, clean pavement.
   (H)   All shared-use electric foot scooters shall be capable of being
remotely locked down by the shared-use electric foot scooter operator upon any
report that the shared-use electric foot scooter is not operating properly, is
unsafe or is stolen, pending a proper evaluation of the scooter.
   (I)   All shared-use electric foot scooters shall be maintained in a
reasonably clean condition and proper working condition.
   (J)   Shared-use electric foot scooter operators may activate their system
and operate between the hours of 6:00 a.m. and 10:00 p.m. The city may elect to
restrict or expand hours of operation as part of electric foot scooter system
license.
(Ord. 5121, passed 5-16-2022) Penalty, see §
73.999
§ 73.037 PUBLIC INFORMATION.
   (A)   Prior to any person’s first use of a shared-use electric foot scooter,
a shared-use electric foot scooter operator shall provide notice to each user
through a city-specific mobile application regarding local regulations
governing legal shared-use electric foot scooter use and parking, as well as
best practices concerning safe and courteous electric foot scooter use.
   (B)   Each shared-use electric foot scooter operator shall develop a city-
specific page on its website that provides, at a minimum, the city’s rules
governing legal shared-use electric foot scooter use and parking, as well as
best practices concerning safe and uncourteous shared-use electric foot scooter
use.
(Ord. 5121, passed 5-16-2022) Penalty, see §
73.999
§ 73.038 REMOVAL AND IMPOUNDMENT.
   (A)   Any shared-use electric foot scooter that is reported to a shared-use
electric foot scooter operator as unsafe to operate or as inoperable shall be
immediately and remotely locked down by the shared-use electric foot scooter
operator, pending a proper evaluation of the scooter and removed from the
public right-of-way within two hours of the shared-use electric foot scooter
operator’s receiving notice of the defect or inoperability.
   (B)   Any unsafe or inoperable shared-use electric foot scooter that is not
removed from the public right-of-way by the shared-use electric foot scooter
operator may be removed and impounded by the city and penalties may be assessed
to the shared-use electric foot scooter operator.
(Ord. 5121, passed 5-16-2022) Penalty, see §
73.999
§ 73.039 LICENSE REVOCATION.
   (A)   A permit issued to a shared-use electric foot scooter operator shall
be issued for a specified time period, subject to revocation. The City Board of
Public Works and Safety may revoke a shared-use electric foot scooter
operator’s license at any time if it finds that one of the following conditions
exists:
      (1)   The shared-use electric foot scooter operator has failed to comply
with the requirements of this subchapter, including, but not limited to,
requirements regarding parking, age of users, scooter condition, scooter
equipment, scooter appearance, maintenance, lock-down, removal or data
reporting;
      (2)   The shared-use electric foot scooter operator has failed to comply
with other reasonable requirements imposed by the City Board of Public Works
and Safety;
      (3)   The shared-use electric foot scooter operator has violated any
federal law or federal regulation; any state law or regulation; or any local
law or regulation;
      (4)   The shared-use electric foot scooter operator submitted false or
inaccurate information in its application; and
      (5)   The shared-use electric foot scooter operator failed to make
payment of required fees or charges.
   (B)   Before the City Board of Public Works and Safety revokes a shared-use
electric foot scooter operator’s license under this section, it shall conduct a
hearing to consider the evidence supporting said revocation. Following the
hearing, the Board shall issue written findings of fact.
(Ord. 5121, passed 5-16-2022)
§ 73.040 ENFORCEMENT.
   The city may enforce violations of this subchapter pursuant to §§
10.35 through
10.40,
Chapter 31 or I.C. 34-28-5, as amended from time to time.
(Ord. 5121, passed 5-16-2022)
BICYCLES, SKATEBOARDS AND THE LIKE
§ 73.055 SKATEBOARDS; OPERATION IN GENERAL.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to operate or ride a skateboard, roller
skates, in-line skates, toy vehicle or other similar device upon a public
street, sidewalk, pedestrian/bicycle trail or public parking area in a manner
that would reasonably result in a loss of control of the device, or cause
damage to property or injury to another.
(Prior Code, § 4.7.2.1) (Ord. 4348, passed 5-4-2006) Penalty, see §
73.999
§ 73.056 SKATEBOARDS; OPERATION IN DOWNTOWN BUSINESS DISTRICT PROHIBITED.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for any person to operate or ride a skateboard,
roller skates, in-line skates, toy vehicle or other similar device upon a
sidewalk or public parking area within the Downtown Business District.
   (B)   For the purposes of this subchapter, the Downtown Business District
shall include the area described below, including the sidewalks and public
parking areas within and adjacent to the streets listed, but excluding
designated pedestrian/bicycle trails within and adjacent to the described
Downtown Business District:
      (1)   Madison Street from Third Street to Fifth Street;
      (2)   Fifth Street from Madison Street to Pike Street;
      (3)   Pike Street from Fifth Street to Second Street;
      (4)   Second Street from Pike Street to Washington Street;
      (5)   Washington Street from Second Street to Third Street; and
      (6)   Third Street from Washington Street to Madison Street.
(Prior Code, § 4.7.2.2) (Ord. 4348, passed 5-4-2006) Penalty, see §
73.999
§ 73.057 SKATEBOARDS; OPERATION ON PRIVATELY-OWNED REAL ESTATE.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to operate or ride a skateboard, roller
skates, in-line skates, toy vehicle or other similar device upon any private
parking area or parts of privately-owned real estate without the real estate
owner’s consent.
(Prior Code, § 4.7.2.3) (Ord. 4348, passed 5-4-2006) Penalty, see §
73.999
§ 73.058 SKATEBOARDS; ENFORCEMENT.
   (A)   Section
73.057 may be enforced on privately-owned real estate where the real estate
owner has:
      (1)   Executed a contract with the city to authorize enforcement by the
City Police Department; and
      (2)   Posted signs within the private parking area or parts of privately-
owned real estate that skateboarding, roller skating and the like is prohibited
and a violation may be enforced by the City Police Department.
   (B)   In all other respects, this subchapter shall be enforced in accordance
with §§
10.35 through
10.40 as adopted in 2004, and as the city code may be amended from time to
time.
(Prior Code, § 4.7.2.4) (Ord. 4348, passed 5-4-2006)
§ 73.999 PENALTY.
   (A)   General penalty. Any person violating any provision of this chapter
for which no specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Off-road vehicles.
      (1)   Violation notices issued under §§
73.001 through 73.005 may be issued by any sworn member of the City Police
Department, any city code compliance officer, any designated parking
enforcement officer or any other duly authorized city employee.
      (2)   Sections
73.001 through 73.005 may be enforced in the City Ordinance Violations Bureau.
A person who violates §§
73.001 through 73.005 shall on the first offense be ordered to pay a civil
penalty in the amount of $175. A person who violates §§
73.001 through 73.005 a second time within a 12-month period shall be ordered
to pay a civil penalty of $225, and a person will be ordered to pay $250 for a
third violation within that 12-month period.
      (3)   The city may enforce §§
73.001 through 73.005 by filing an action in any court of general jurisdiction.
         (a)   Any person who violates §
73.003(A)(1) will be deemed to have committed an ordinance violation and may be
fined as if the person had violated I.C. 9-13-2-123, but in no case be fined
more than a maximum of $2,500.
         (b)   Any person who violates §
73.003(A)(3) will be deemed to have committed an ordinance violation and may be
fined as if the person had violated I.C. 9-21-9-2 but in no case be fined more
than a maximum of $2,500.
         (c)   Any person who violates §
73.003(D) will be deemed to have committed an ordinance violation and may be
fined as if the person had violated I.C. 9-25-8-2 but in no case be fined more
than a maximum of $2,500.
         (d)   Any person who violates any provision of §§
73.001 through 73.005 for which a penalty is not otherwise provided will be
deemed to have committed an ordinance violation and may be fined not more than
$500 for each offense.
      (4)   A person who violates §§
73.001 through 73.005 may be subject to the impoundment of the off-road
vehicle.
      (5)   All fines or civil penalties collected shall be deposited in the
General Fund of the city.
   (C)   Golf carts.
      (1)   Violation notices issued under §§
73.015 through
73.018 may be issued by any sworn member of the City Police Department, any
city code enforcement officer, any designated parking enforcement officer or
any other duly authorized city inspector or designated code enforcement agent,
or assistant.
      (2)   Sections
73.015 through
73.018 may be enforced in the City Ordinance Violations Bureau. A person who
violates §§
73.015 through
73.018 shall, on the first offense, be ordered to pay a civil penalty in the
amount of $175. A person who violates §§
73.015 through
73.018 a second time within a 12-month period shall be ordered to pay a civil
penalty of $225, and a person will be ordered to pay $250 for a third violation
within that 12-month period.
      (3)   The city may enforce §§
73.015 through
73.018 by filing an action in any court of general jurisdiction to recover a
fine in a sum not to exceed $2,500.
      (4)   A person who violates §§
73.015 through
73.018 may be subject to the impoundment of the golf cart and/or revocation of
a permit issued under §§
73.015 through
73.018.
      (5)   All fines or civil penalties collected shall be deposited in the
General Fund of the city.
   (D)   Bicycles, skateboards and the like. Any person who violates §§ 73.050
through
73.058 is subject to a fine of up to $500.
(Prior Code, § 4.7.2.5)
(Ord. 4348, passed 5-4-2006; Ord. 5192, passed 7-11-2024; Ord. 5221, passed 5-
20-2025)
CHAPTER 74: TRAFFIC SCHEDULES
Schedule
      
I.   Commercial vehicles
SCHEDULE I. COMMERCIAL VEHICLES.
   (A)   Prohibition. In accordance with I.C. 9-20-1-3(c), trucks or commercial
vehicles in excess of 20 feet in length are prohibited from operating on Main
Street from Pike Street/U.S. 33 to Madison Street except as necessary to make
deliveries on Main Street in the prohibited area. The length of the vehicle
includes the length of the truck and trailer.
   (B)   Penalty.
      (1)   Any person found violating this schedule shall be subject to
enforcement in accordance with I.C. 34-28-5 et seq. and I.C. 9-30-3 et seq., as
each may be amended from time to time.
      (2)   The court may enter a judgment for violation of this section, in an
amount not exceeding $2,500.
   (C)   Exempted vehicles. This section does not apply to vehicles used solely
for personal use; vehicles owned or operated on behalf of federal, state and
local governments; or buses and trolleys that are 45 feet or less.
   (D)   Signs. Appropriate signs shall be posted designating the size of
trucks and commercial vehicles that are prohibited from using Main Street from
Pike Street/U.S. 33 to Madison Street.
   (E)   Commercial vehicle. Commercial vehicle has the meaning set forth in 49
C.F.R. § 383.5.
(Ord. 5045, passed 7-9-2020)

GENERAL PROVISIONS
§ 90.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   DOMESTIC ANIMALS. Dogs, cats, parakeets, canaries and other small animals or
birds which are normally kept within the living quarters of an abode.
   LIVESTOCK and FOWL. All animals and fowl that provide food and/or are beasts
of burden, such as cattle of all species, cows, bulls, horses, mules, ponies,
burros, goats, sheep, hogs, chickens, ducks and any other animals of a similar
kind, nature, type or use.
   WILD ANIMALS.
      (1)   All poisonous reptiles or any other dangerous or carnivorous wild
animal or reptile, any vicious or dangerous domesticated animals or any other
animal or reptile of wild, vicious or dangerous propensities.
      (2)   This term shall include, but not be restricted to, apes, bears,
constrictor snakes over six feet in length, coyotes, deer, foxes, gamecocks and
other fighting birds, monkeys and wolves.
(Prior Code, § 3.1.2.4) (Ord. 432, passed 12-19-1921; Ord. 905, passed 11-4-
1940; Ord. 1419, passed 1-6-1958; Ord. 2805, passed 8-6-1981; Ord. 5265, passed
6-22-2026)
§ 90.02 RUNNING AT LARGE.
   (A)   No person shall suffer, permit or allow any animal or any dog, cat or
other domestic animal or pet to run at large within the city limits. For
purposes of definition, any such animal or dog, cat or other domestic animal or
pet which is untethered shall be determined to be RUNNING AT LARGE.
   (B)   All animals found running at large shall be impounded.
   (C)   The owner of any animal shall be responsible for any damages to the
person or property of others caused by said animal while running at large.
(Prior Code, § 3.1.1.1) (Ord. 1477, passed 3-7-1960; Ord. 2373, passed 10-5-
1976; Ord. 5265, passed 6-22-2026) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.03 EXPOSED TO DISEASE.
   All animals which are afflicted with a contagious or infectious disease and
have been found at large, or have come into contact with, or exposed to any
public place, shall be placed in an animal shelter provided for that purpose by
contract or otherwise by the city.
(Prior Code, § 3.1.1.2) (Ord. 1477, passed 3-7-1960; Ord. 5265, passed 6-22-
2026)
§ 90.04 KEEPING LIVESTOCK, FOWL OR WILD ANIMALS.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to have, keep or otherwise maintain
within the corporate limits of the city, any livestock, fowl or wild animal
excepting domestic animals, unless such livestock, fowl or wild animal is
confined not less than 150 feet from the residence of any other person;
provided, however, that no such livestock, fowl or wild animal shall be so kept
or maintained if noxious and/or offensive odors are caused to enter upon and
cross the real estate owned and/or occupied by another; provided further that
any animal of any type may be kept in a zoological garden which must be set up
in a prescribed area zoned for such purposes; provided further that chickens
may be kept, possessed or maintained only in accordance with §§
90.35 through
90.41.
(Prior Code, § 3.1.1.3) (Ord. 432, passed 12-19-1921; Ord. 905, passed 11-4-
1940; Ord. 2805, passed 1-6-1958; Ord. 4773, passed 4-17-2014; Ord. 5265,
passed 6-22-2026) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.05 HARBORING VICIOUS ANIMAL.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for any person to keep or harbor any cross,
vicious or unruly animal.
   (B)   The members of the Police Department are authorized to kill any
dangerous or vicious animal when it is necessary for the protection of any
person or property.
(Prior Code, § 3.1.1.4) (Ord. 149, passed 9-14-1903; Ord. 1477, passed 3-7-
1960; Ord. 5265, passed 6-22-2026) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.06 ANIMALS DISTURBING THE PEACE.
   No person, firm or corporation owning or harboring any dog, cat or other
domestic animal or pet shall suffer or permit such animal to disturb the peace
or quiet of the neighborhood by barking or making other loud or unusual noise.
(Prior Code, § 3.1.1.5) (Ord. 1477, passed 3-7-1960; Ord. 2373, passed 10-5-
1976; Ord. 5265, passed 6-22-2026) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.07 NOXIOUS ODORS.
   (A)   No person shall have, keep or otherwise harbor any dog, cat or other
domestic animal or pet in the city if noxious and/or offensive odors are
thereby caused to enter upon or across the real estate owned or occupied by
another or upon or across any public right-of-way.
   (B)   It shall be the duty of the owner of the property where any violation
exists to eliminate the violation by removing the substances, materials or
animal excrements giving rise to any odor and to maintain the premises free
from such unwholesome substances. Should the owner, upon proper notice in
writing, fail or refuse to remove the violation, the city may enter upon the
premises where the violation exists and abate the violation by removing the
substances, materials or animal excrement giving rise to the offensive odors
and by impounding the animal found on the premises. The animal so impounded
shall be placed in an animal shelter provided for that purpose.
(Prior Code, § 3.1.1.6) (Ord. 2373, passed 10-5-1976; Ord. 5265, passed 6-22-
2026) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.08 SECURING ANIMALS.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for any person to leave unattended, any horse or
other animal which is hitched to any vehicle in any of the streets or alleys
without securely fastening them to some object to prevent them from running
away.
   (B)   It shall be unlawful for any person to tie any horse or other animal
within said city without securing the same so that it cannot go upon or over
any sidewalk.
   (C)   It shall be unlawful to hitch any horse or other animal or animals to
any shade tree upon or along any street or alley.
(Prior Code, § 3.1.1.7) (Ord. 149, passed 9-14-1903; Ord. 5265, passed 6-22-
2026) Penalty, see §
90.99
IMPOUNDMENT
§ 90.20 REDEMPTION.
   The owner of any animals impounded pursuant to this chapter, within three
days after the impoundment redeem the animal by paying all the expenses
incident to impounding such animal, caring for the animal and paying any
lawfully imposed and unpaid license fees for such animals that have accrued up
to the time of redemption. It shall be the duty of such impounding authority to
release such animal to the owner.
(Prior Code, § 3.1.2.1) (Ord. 1477, passed 3-7-1960; Ord. 5265, passed 6-22-
2026)
§ 90.21 DISPOSITION WHEN NOT REDEEMED.
   Any animal impounded pursuant to the provisions of this chapter which is not
redeemed by its owner within five days after it has been impounded may, at the
election of the impounding supervisor, be sold or destroyed in a humane manner.
(Prior Code, § 3.1.2.2) (Ord. 1477, passed 3-7-1960; Ord. 5265, passed 6-22-
2026)
§ 90.22 VACCINATION OF DOGS REQUIRED.
   No dog impounded for running at large shall be released to any person unless
said dog shall, prior to such release, have received vaccination for rabies,
and the person receiving said animal upon release has paid the cost of such
vaccination.
(Prior Code, § 3.1.2.3) (Ord. 1477, passed 3-7-1960; Ord. 5265, passed 6-22-
2026)
KEEPING CHICKENS
§ 90.35 APPLICATION OF CHAPTER.
   This subchapter does not apply to any real estate that is zoned agricultural
or to any real estate that has been granted a zoning variance that specifically
permits the raising of chickens on the real estate.
(Prior Code, § 3.1.4.1) (Ord. 4773, passed 4-17-2014; Ord. 4849, passed 11-20-
2015)
§ 90.36 KEEPING CHICKENS.
   It shall be unlawful for a person to keep, possess or maintain chickens in
violation of this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 3.1.4.2) (Ord. 4773, passed 4-17-2014; Ord. 4849, passed 11-20-
2015) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.37 CHICKENS PERMITTED.
   (A)   Chickens shall be permitted only upon:
      (1)   Real estate containing an occupied single-family dwelling;
      (2)   Real estate containing a duplex, provided the owner of the real
estate resides in one of the two units; or
      (3)   Each residential lot of a multi-family dwelling with the owner’s
permission.
   (B)   Any person who intends to keep, possess or maintain chickens must
obtain a permit from the city. The permit will be issued without cost.
(Prior Code, § 3.1.4.3) (Ord. 4773, passed 4-17-2014; Ord. 4849, passed 11-20-
2015; Ord. 4987, passed 11-26-2018) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.38 LIMITATIONS.
   (A)   No more than six chickens may be located on any residential real
estate lot. Roosters and other types of fowl are prohibited.
   (B)   Chickens shall be kept for pets or personal, non-commercial use only.
Selling of eggs and fertilizer is prohibited.
   (C)   Breeding of chickens is prohibited on the premises.
   (D)   Slaughtering of chickens is prohibited on the premises.
   (E)   A chicken may not be turned loose or taken to the humane shelter.
(Prior Code, § 3.1.4.4) (Ord. 4773, passed 4-17-2014; Ord. 4849, passed 11-20-
2015; Ord. 4987, passed 11-26-2018) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.39 CHICKEN ENCLOSURE.
   (A)   All chickens shall be kept outside of the dwelling in a chicken
enclosure which shall include a chicken coop and a covered chicken pen/run.
      (1)   The chicken enclosure shall be secure to prevent the entry of
rodents and predators, and shall effectively contain and protect the chickens.
      (2)   The chicken enclosure shall be located at least 15 feet from the
property lines, and at least 20 feet from any adjacent residential dwelling,
church, school or place of business. The chicken enclosure may not be located
in the front yard of the residential dwelling as defined by § 90.42. The
chicken enclosure may be located in a yard adjacent to a street that is not
considered a front yard if the location meets the front yard setbacks
established by the Zoning Ordinance in Appendix A to Title XV.
      (3)   The chicken coop shall be constructed of solid materials on all
sides, including roof and door(s). Vents, covered with wire, shall be placed as
necessary for adequate ventilation. The chicken coop shall provide at least two
square feet of area per chicken.
      (4)   The chicken pen/run shall be connected to and/or surround the
chicken coop and be constructed of traditional building materials or fencing.
The pen/run shall provide at least two square feet of area per chicken, not
exceed six feet in height, and shall be covered with wire, aviary netting or
solid roofing.
      (5)   The chicken coop, pen and run shall be constructed and maintained
in a workmanlike manner and comply with all applicable City Building Code
requirements for accessory structures as may be amended from time to time.
   (B)   The chicken enclosure and surrounding area shall be maintained in a
clean, dry, odor free and sanitary condition at all times. All manure, uneaten
feed and other trash shall be regularly removed and disposed of in a sanitary
manner so that the chicken enclosure and surrounding area does not constitute a
nuisance, safety hazard or health problem to surrounding property.
(Prior Code, § 3.1.4.5) (Ord. 4773, passed 4-17-2014; Ord. 4849, passed 11-20-
2015) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.40 DISPOSAL.
   Those who wish to dispose of their hen(s) must transport them to a licensed
processor for butchering, take them to a veterinarian’s office for euthanizing
or place the hen(s) in a new home. The new home must include the new owner’s
permission and the new placement must meet all criteria of this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 3.1.4.6) (Ord. 4773, passed 4-17-2014; Ord. 4849, passed 11-20-
2015) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.41 FRONT YARD OF RESIDENTIAL DWELLING.
   The FRONT YARD OF RESIDENTIAL DWELLING is defined for purposes of this
subchapter as the area between property line and the foundation of the
residence extending across the full width of the lot on the side of the
residence adjacent to the street. If the residential lot is adjacent to more
than one street, the front yard shall be the yard adjacent to the street which
is the street address of the residence.
(Prior Code, § 3.1.4.8) (Ord. 4773, passed 4-17-2014; Ord. 4849, passed 11-20-
2015)
§ 90.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   (1)   Any person who violates the provisions set forth in §§
90.01 through
90.08 and
90.20 through
90.22 shall be fined in a sum of not less than $5 nor more than $200, and each
day’s continuance shall constitute a separate offense.
      (2)   All monies received pursuant to §§
90.01 through
90.08 and
90.20 through
90.22 shall be paid to the Clerk-Treasurer who shall place such monies in the
General Fund of the city.
(Prior Code, § 3.1.3.1)
   (B)   Sections
90.35 through
90.41 shall be enforced in accordance with the applicable provisions of §§
10.35 through
10.40, as may be amended from time to time.
(Prior Code, § 3.1.4.7)
(Ord. 1477, passed 3-7-1960; Ord. 2373, passed 10-5-1976; Ord. 4773, passed 4-
17-2014; Ord. 4849, passed 11-20-2015)

   Sections
91.001 through

91.090 through

“Fire Prevention Ordinance of Goshen, Indiana” (“Ordinance”).
(Ord. 5188, passed 6-10-2024)
§ 91.002 PURPOSE.
   The purpose of §§
91.001 through
91.008,
91.020 through
91.031,
91.045,
91.046,
91.060 through
91.064,
91.075 through
91.077 and
91.090 through
91.093 is to protect the life, public safety, health and general welfare of the
citizens of the city, and shall be construed in such a manner as to effectuate
this purpose.
(Ord. 5188, passed 6-10-2024)

administer and enforce the following:
   (A)   All provisions of §§

91.031,
91.045,

   (B)   Variances granted in accordance with I.C. 22-13-2-11; and
   (C)   Orders issued under I.C. 22-12-7.
(Ord. 5188, passed 6-10-2024)

91.075 through

for the promotion and protection of the safety and welfare of the public
applies.
(Ord. 5188, passed 6-10-2024)
§ 91.006 MINIMUM STANDARDS.
   (A)   All rules of the State Fire Prevention and Building Safety Commission
as set out in Articles 12, 13, 18, 22 and 25 of Title 675 of the Indiana
Administrative Code are incorporated in §§
91.001 through
91.008,
91.020 through
91.031,
91.045,
91.046,
91.060 through
91.064,
91.075 through
91.077 and
91.090 through
91.093 and shall include all later amendments to that article as published in
the Indiana Register or the Indiana Administrative Code with effective dates as
fixed therein.
   (B)   Any special processes or procedures not addressed in the State Fire
Code (675 I.A.C. 22) or §§
91.001 through
91.008,
91.020 through
91.031,
91.045,
91.046,
91.060 through
91.064,
91.075 through
91.077 and
91.090 through

recognized by State Fire Code (675 I.A.C. 22), Referenced Standards and as
approved by the Fire Chief, or the Fire Chief’s designee.
   (C)   Any special processes or procedures not addressed in §§
91.001 through
91.008,
