§ 151.11 REVIEW OF APPLICATION.
   Prior to the issuance of any building permit, the Building Department shall:
   (A)   Review all building permit applications to determine full compliance
with the provisions of this subchapter;
   (B)   Review all building permit applications for new construction or
substantial improvements to determine whether proposed building sites will be
reasonably safe from flooding;
   (C)   Review all building permit applications for major repairs within the
floodplain area having special flood hazards to determine that the proposed
repair:
      (1)   Used construction materials and utility equipment that are
resistant to flood damage; and
      (2)   Used construction methods and practices that will minimize flood
damage.
   (D)   Review all building permit applications for new construction or
substantial improvements within the floodplain area having special flood
hazards to assure that the proposed construction (including prefabricated and
mobile homes):
      (1)   Is protected against flood damage;
      (2)   Is designed (or modified) and anchored to prevent flotation,
collapse or lateral movement of the structure, flood damage; and
      (3)   Used construction methods and practices that will minimize flood
damage.
(Prior Code, § 6.1.1.11) (Ord. 3714, passed 4-9-1996)
§ 151.12 INSPECTION.
   After the issuance of any building permit, the Building Commissioner shall
make, or shall cause to be made, inspections of the work being done as are
necessary to ensure full compliance with the provisions of this subchapter and
the terms of the permit. Reinspection of work found to be incomplete or not
ready for inspection is subject to assessment of reinspection fees as
prescribed by the current Building Department Fee Ordinance.
(Prior Code, § 6.1.1.12) (Ord. 3714, passed 4-9-1996)
§ 151.13 INSPECTION ASSISTANCE.
   The Chief of the Fire Department, or his or her designated representative,
shall assist the Building Commissioner in the inspection of fire suppression,
detection and alarm systems, and shall provide reports of such inspections to
the Building Commissioner.
(Prior Code, § 6.1.1.13) (Ord. 3714, passed 4-9-1996)
§ 151.14 ENTRY.
   Upon presentation of proper credentials, the Building Commissioner, or his
or her duly authorized representative, may enter any building, structure or
premises in the city to perform any duty imposed upon him or her by this code.
Such entry, unless prior arrangements have been made, shall be between 7:00
a.m. and 6:30 p.m., Monday through Saturday.
(Prior Code, § 6.1.1.14) (Ord. 3714, passed 4-9-1996)
§ 151.15 STOP ORDER.
   Whenever any work is being done contrary to the provisions of this
subchapter, the Building Commissioner may order the work stopped by notice in
writing served on any persons engaged in doing or causing such work to be done.
Such persons shall, without delay, stop such work until authorized by the
Building Commissioner to proceed.
(Prior Code, § 6.1.1.15) (Ord. 3714, passed 4-9-1996)
§ 151.16 CERTIFICATE OF OCCUPANCY.
   No certificate of occupancy for any building or structure constructed,
altered or repaired after the adoption of this code shall be issued unless such
building or structure was constructed, altered or repaired in compliance with
the provisions of this subchapter. It shall be unlawful to occupy any such
building or structure unless a full, partial or temporary certificate of
occupancy has been issued by the Building Commissioner.
(Prior Code, § 6.1.1.16) (Ord. 3714, passed 4-9-1996) Penalty, see §
151.99
§ 151.17 WORKMANSHIP.
   All work on the construction, alteration and repair of any building or
structure shall be performed in a good and workmanlike manner according to
accepted standards and practices in the trade.
(Prior Code, § 6.1.1.17) (Ord. 3714, passed 4-9-1996) Penalty, see §
151.99
§ 151.18 VIOLATIONS.
   Any person, partnership, corporation or other entity commits a violation of
this subchapter if it does any of the following:
   (A)   Erects, constructs, enlarges, alters, repairs, improves, removes,
converts, demolishes or equips any building or structure in the city in
violation of the provisions of this code or any code adopted by reference in
this subchapter;
   (B)   Permits another person or entity to erect, construct, enlarge, alter,
repair, improve, remove, convert, demolish or equip any building or structure
in the city in violation of the provisions of this code or any code adopted by
reference in this subchapter;
   (C)   Fails to obtain a permit required under this subchapter prior to the
commencement of the construction, alteration or repair of any building or
structure;
   (D)   Fails to comply with any valid order of the Building Commissioner
within the time period prescribed by the Building Commissioner;
   (E)   Fails to perform any duty established by this subchapter; and
   (F)   Takes any action prohibited by this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 6.1.1.18) (Ord. 3714, passed 4-9-1996) Penalty, see §
151.99
§ 151.19 RIGHT OF APPEAL.
   All persons shall have the right to appeal the Building Commissioner’s
decision. The appeal will initially be heard by a five member panel known as
the Board of Building Appeals. The Fire Chief of the city shall be one member
of the panel and shall act as secretary to the panel. Two members are to be
appointed to two-year terms by the Mayor of the city, and two members are to be
appointed to two-year terms by the Common Council. All shall be qualified in
the area of building construction by reasons of their education and/or
experience. Members will serve with no remuneration from the city. Appeals will
be heard first through the Board of Building Appeals and then the State Fire
Prevention and Building Safety Commission in accordance with the provisions of
I.C. 22-13-2-7 and I.C. 4-21.5-3-7. Any appeal from the Building Commissioner’s
decision shall be made within 15 days of that decision.
(Prior Code, § 6.1.1.19) (Ord. 3714, passed 4-9-1996)
§ 151.20 REMEDIES.
   The city may bring an action for injunctive relief to enforce all applicable
building codes adopted in this subchapter by reference, to secure compliance
with any order made by the Building Commissioner or to prosecute a violation of
this subchapter in any court of competent jurisdiction within the county.
(Prior Code, § 6.1.1.20) (Ord. 3714, passed 4-9-1996)
§ 151.21 SUSPENSION OR REVOCATION.
   The Building Commissioner may, in writing, suspend or revoke a permit issued
under the provisions of this subchapter whenever the permit is issued in error
on the basis of incorrect information supplied or if it is issued in violation
of any ordinance, regulation or any provision of this subchapter or amendments
thereto.
(Prior Code, § 6.1.1.22) (Ord. 3714, passed 4-9-1996)
§ 151.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   (1)   A person, partnership, corporation or other entity who violates
the provisions of §§
151.01 through
151.21 is subject to the following:
         (a)   A $500 fine for each violation. Each day that a violation
continues shall constitute a separate violation; and
         (b)   The revocation of the right to take out permits within the city
for a period of up to two years for the violation of this §§
151.01 through
151.21, provided that a court of competent jurisdiction has found the same
person, partnership, corporation or other entity to have violated a provision
of §§
151.01 through
151.21 within the previous two years.
      (2)   Division (B)(1)(b) above shall not be construed to limit, in any
way, the city’s right to take action to revoke a license under any other
applicable section of this code, amendments thereto, other city ordinance or
state statute.
(Prior Code, § 6.1.1.21) (Ord. 3714, passed 4-9-1996)
CHAPTER 152: CONTRACTORS
Section
Mechanical and Electrical Contractors
   
152.01   Title
   
152.02   Definitions
   
152.03   Application for license and licensing requirements
   
152.04   License renewal and registration requirements
   
152.05   Violations
   
152.06   Enforcement
 
   
152.99   Penalty
MECHANICAL AND ELECTRICAL CONTRACTORS
§ 152.01 TITLE.
   This subchapter shall be more commonly known as the “Licensing and
Registering of Contractors Ordinance of the City of Goshen, Indiana”.
(Ord. 5258, passed 6-22-2026)
§ 152.02 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   CLASS A MECHANICAL CONTRACTOR. A person who, for hire, does himself or
herself, or by employing others on his or her behalf, erects, installs, alters,
repairs, relocates, replaces, maintains, designs or performs any other work to
any heating, ventilating, cooling, refrigeration system and other miscellaneous
heat-producing appliances, including all appurtenances, apparatus or equipment
used in connection therewith. A CLASS A MECHANICAL CONTRACTOR, however, may not
make the electrical connection from the warm air heating unit, air-conditioning
unit or wet/dry heat pump into the main service box.
   CLASS B MECHANICAL CONTRACTOR. A person who, for hire, does himself or
herself, or by employing others on his or her behalf, installs, alters,
repairs, relocates, replaces, maintains or performs any other work to any
heating, ventilating or cooling system, including all appurtenances, apparatus
or equipment used in connection therewith exclusively in any mobile home
dwelling unit. A CLASS B MECHANICAL CONTRACTOR, however, may not make the
electrical connection from the warm air heating unit, air-conditioning unit or
wet/dry heat pump into the main service box.
   ELECTRICAL CONTRACTOR. A person who, for hire, does himself or herself, or
by employing others on his or her behalf, installs, alters, repairs, replaces,
designs or performs any work to an electrical system.
   LICENSEE. A person who holds an electrical contractor license, Class A
mechanical contractor license or Class B mechanical contractor license, and is
registered with the City Building Department pursuant to this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 6.2.1.9) (Ord. 5258, passed 6-22-2026)
§ 152.03 APPLICATION FOR LICENSE AND LICENSING REQUIREMENTS.
   (A)   A person acting in the capacity of an electrical contractor, Class A
mechanical contractor or Class B mechanical contractor in the city shall be
required to first obtain the appropriate license and register with the City
Building Department.
   (B)   To be licensed as an electrical contractor, Class A mechanical
contractor or Class B mechanical contractor in the city, a person must:
      (1)   Be at least 18 years of age.
      (2)   File with the City Building Department a written application, on a
form provided by the Building Department, that contains information considered
necessary to determine the qualifications of the applicant including evidence
of the applicant’s training, experience and past work record. The City Building
Commissioner may investigate the statements and information included in the
application, and if the Building Commissioner finds any statements or
information to be untrue, the Building Commissioner may refuse to approve the
application and issue a license.
      (3)   Successfully complete an examination approved by the City Building
Commissioner. The contents of the examination must, for each license category,
test the current level of skills required of persons to be licensed in that
category. A passing score on the examination shall be a score of at least 70%.
However, if the applicant holds a license for the license category from another
locality, a license for that license category may be granted in the city
without examination, provided that the requirements for the licensing of an
electrical contractor, Class A mechanical contractor or Class B mechanical
contractor in the other locality are equal to or greater than the licensing
requirements of the city, as determined in the sole discretion of the City
Building Commissioner.
   (C)   Upon filing an application, the person must pay a non-refundable
application fee in the amount of $50. A separate application is required for
each license category.
(Prior Code, § 6.2.1.2) (Ord. 5258, passed 6-22-2026)
§ 152.04 LICENSE RENEWAL AND REGISTRATION REQUIREMENTS.
   (A)   The City Building Commissioner shall issue a license to and register a
person who meets the requirements under §
152.03.
   (B)   Any license granted under this subchapter shall be prominently
displayed at the licensee’s principal place of business.
   (C)   Upon the person’s payment of a license and registration fee in the
amount of $400, the City Building Commissioner will issue a license with an
initial term valid for the remaining portion of the then current calendar year,
plus two additional calendar years.
   (D)   A licensee in good standing shall have the right, without further
examination, to renew their license and registration by filing a renewal
application with the City Building Department. Upon receipt of the renewal
application and the licensee’s payment of a license and registration fee in the
amount of $400, the City Building Commissioner will issue a renewal license and
registration. The renewal license and registration shall be valid for a period
of three calendar years.
   (E)   In the event a licensee fails to renew their license and registration
and pay the required fee within 30 days after the expiration of the initial
term or any renewal term, the licensee shall pay a late fee in the amount of
$150 and the license and registration will be renewed. However, if a license
and registration have been expired for six months or more, the person’s license
is forfeited and the person must reapply for the license pursuant to §
152.03.
   (F)   If the Building Commissioner refuses to issue a license and register a
person under this subchapter, the person whose application was denied may
appeal the decision to the Board of Public Works and Safety, and a hearing will
be held before the Board within 30 days.
(Prior Code, § 6.2.1.5) (Ord. 5258, passed 6-22-2026)
§ 152.05 VIOLATIONS.
   (A)   It shall be a violation of this subchapter for any person to act in
the capacity of an electrical contractor, Class A mechanical contractor or
Class B mechanical contractor in the city without first obtaining the
appropriate license and registering with the City Building Department.
   (B)   It shall be a violation of this subchapter for any person who fails to
renew the individual’s license and continues to act in the capacity of an
electrical contractor, Class A mechanical contractor or Class B mechanical
contractor in the city after the license has expired.
   (C)   It shall be a violation of this subchapter for any person to continue
to act in the capacity of an electrical contractor, Class A mechanical
contractor or Class B mechanical contractor in the city after the person’s
license has been suspended, revoked or otherwise restricted pursuant to this
subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 6.2.1.7) (Ord. 5258, passed 6-22-2026) Penalty, see §
152.99
§ 152.06 ENFORCEMENT.
   (A)   If the Building Commissioner believes that the license of any
electrical contractor, Class A mechanical contractor or Class B mechanical
contractor should be suspended, revoked or otherwise restricted, the Building
Commissioner may request that the Board of Public Works and Safety take action
by issuing an order to:
      (1)   Suspend the licensee’s license;
      (2)   Revoke the licensee’s license; or
      (3)   Restrict the licensee’s license as deemed appropriate by the Board
of Public Works and Safety.
   (B)   The Board of Public Works and Safety may take action only after a
notice is given to the person whom the order is specifically directed, and a
hearing is conducted at least ten days after the date notice is deemed to be
given.
      (1)   Notice to the individual shall be deemed sufficient if given by:
         (a)   Sending a copy of the order by registered or certified mail to
the residence, place of business or employment of the individual to be notified
with return receipt requested;
         (b)   Delivering a copy of the order personally to the individual to
be notified;
         (c)   Leaving a copy of the order at the residence or usual place of
abode of the individual to be notified; or
         (d)   Sending a copy of the order by first class mail to the last
known address of the person to be notified.
      (2)   If a notice described in subdivision (B)(1)(a) is returned
undelivered, a copy of the order must be given in accordance with subdivision
(B)(1)(b), (B)(1)(c) or (B)(1)(d).
      (3)   At the hearing, the person to whom an order has been issued shall
be given the opportunity to appear with or without legal counsel, present
evidence, cross-examine witnesses and present arguments.
   (C)   Any person aggrieved by a decision of the Building Commissioner or any
action of the Board of Public Works and Safety, in refusing to issue,
suspending, revoking or otherwise restricting a license and registration may
appeal the decision to any court of competent jurisdiction in the county within
30 days from the date of the Board’s action.
   (D)   The city may bring action to enforce the provisions of this subchapter
by filing a complaint in any court of competent jurisdiction within the county.
(Prior Code, § 6.2.1.8) (Ord. 5258, passed 6-22-2026)
§ 152.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person violating any provision of §§
152.01 through
152.06  may be fined an amount not to exceed $2,500, in addition to any court
costs, including reasonable attorneys’ fees. Each day that a violation occurs
or continues shall be deemed a separate offense of §§
152.01 through
152.106.
(Prior Code, § 6.2.1.8) (Ord. 5258, passed 6-22-2026)
